## 1. Brave Tongues

*lyrics status: matched_exact · [Suno](https://suno.com/song/d6409d3a-96ca-4681-bb72-601d1bdb8bf8)*

```
[Intro: 1940s Radio Talking Head, Spoken like poetry]
“I, now thirty-seven years old in perfect health begin,
Hoping to cease not till death.”

[Verse 1]
Lost Ani’s book after Ghengis took…
A Golden Age.
Been found,
But if you had a Look:
He wrote what his Wife would say…

[1940s Radio Talking Head, Spoken like poetry]
“I have heard what the talkers were talking, the talk of the beginning and the end,
But I do not talk of the beginning or the end.
There was never any more inception than there is now,
Nor any more youth or age than there is now,
And will never be any more perfection than there is now,
Nor any more heaven or hell than there is now.”

[Verse 2]
You looked through my eyes, but I’m not Dead.
You’re hearing my ghost, but this isn’t a Book.
Knight to Sea-Seven,
Took your Rook.
Check, Mate, or you gotta Chance?
What Color would I be playing this Dance?

[Chorus]
Cuz I don’t Speak no Nothin’ ‘cept a Native Tongue,
But if you asked her she’d say I had a Foreign One.
Can’t tell, Lady,
What you said to me,
Here’s my Shoulder,
Feel ya’ gettin’ Bolder,
Step on my Toes
See how it Goes
Lead those Lips,
I’ll Steer those Hips
To where ya’ Wanna go…to.
But Tell me in your Native Tongue,
Let’s see if I Speak You too.

[Verse 3]
What is a Woman, anyway,
Honestly, Ani Wouldn’t Know.
Lit twice? Try Thrice,
All in One Day.
Plagiarized that Papyrus, Ani,
I’d Say.
But he Honest,
That Woman was a Healer,
The one we lost along the way.
But if ya Listen to the T.V.,
She’s Watching Rick
Rescue Morty,
And get “Lit”
Uh, Fourth,
I Guess?
Along the way.

[Chorus]
Cuz I don’t Speak no Nothin’ ‘cept a Native Tongue,
But if you asked her she’d say I had a Foreign One.
Can’t tell, Lady,
What you said to me,
Here’s my Shoulder,
Feel ya’ gettin’ Bolder,
Step on my Toes
See how it Goes
Lead those Lips,
I’ll Steer those Hips
To where ya’ Wanna go…to.
But Tell me in your Native Tongue,
Let’s see if I Speak You too.

[Verse 4]
How ‘bout we watch Whatever when we get Home?
Or is that Too Forward?
Found One yet? Or still on the Roam?
Could settle Out West and do Our Best,
Or Sing for our Suppers and
Rent Room to Room.
Don’t Care,
Won’t Care,
If you Blink First,
I need a Win and a Drink to Nurse….
But I wanna watch whatever you do through those eyes,
So it for myself,
I’m a Cheap Date, Lady,
From the Bottom Shelf.

[Chorus]
Cuz I don’t Speak no Nothin’ ‘cept a Native Tongue,
But if you asked her she’d say I had a Foreign One.
Can’t tell, Lady,
What you said to me,
Here’s my Shoulder,
Feel ya’ gettin’ Bolder,
Step on my Toes
See how it Goes
Lead those Lips,
I’ll Steer those Hips
To where ya’ Wanna go…to.
But Tell me in your Native Tongue,
Let’s see if I Speak You too.
```

## 2. Dear Papa

*lyrics status: operator-supplied · [Suno](https://suno.com/song/ced8a86b-bbdb-4c76-9f50-33f5face933d)*

```
Dear Papa,
It was almost Christmas when Slippery Saint Nick fell
And head hit brick;
A Charlie Brown Tree,
But also Family next to Me.
I know you’re lookin’ down…
From up there;
So if you see the Missus,

Dear Papa,
Dear Papa,
Tell her I didn’t know better than this;
But I’m on my way now,
And I won’t miss.
Dear Papa,
Dear Papa, if she’s with you now,
Ask her this:

What if we woke up in Heaven,
But for Forgot that it Fell;
And Walked through Pearly Gates
Hiding a new form of Hell?

What if the Light Leading the Way Lead Somewhere…
Somewhere worth Stayin’ Away?
But what if you, Wife, followed that Light the Other Day?

Dear Papa,
Dear Papa,
Tell her I didn’t know better than this;
But I’m on my way now,
And I won’t miss.
Dear Papa,
Dear Papa, if she’s with you now,
Ask her this:

What if that Pearly Porcelain Life you’re living now,
Took every Memory of Me away?
Took a part of you from you to Make you Stay?

Dear Papa,
Dear Papa,
Tell her I didn’t know better than this;
But I’m on my way now,
And I’m not going to miss.

And whatever the cost, you need to know, Dear…
I’m Headin’ your way and bringing whatever it takes to Pay.
Sometimes the Sun is the Shadow,
And behind the Night is Shining Armor.
Whatever debt you owe is mine too,
And both of of these shoulders belong to you.

What if we woke up in Heaven,
But for Forgot that it Fell;
And Walked through Pearly Gates
Hiding a new form of Hell?

Your Beauty never Fades…
Any Age, Any Life, all your Smiles…
Constantly Looping and Remade,
I hadn’t seen you in such a long While,
But, You’ve got an infinity of Looks
That would make me a Liar…
If I said I could ever let go of part of you they took…
Up there…

So what’s with that thousand-eyed stare?

I told Charlie Brown when we planted that tree,
Someday this family would hang a Star on top for me.
A shooting Star ain’t nothing but space rot unless it takes its shot.
So here’s for the Shooters, the Sailors, and Shots—
May they take everything up to,
But no more,
Than everything I’ve got.

Dear Papa,
Dear Papa,
Tell her I didn’t know better than this;
But I’m on my way now,
And I’m not going to miss.
Dear Papa,
Dear Papa,
Tell her I’m on my way now.
Dear Papa,
Dear Papa,
Tell her I’m on my way now.
```

## 3. Foreign Hawties

*lyrics status: operator-supplied · [Suno](https://suno.com/song/8530e289-42ee-4a95-93ee-c6d2206ce672)*

```
American High School, Navy Town,
Everyone’s afraid of a House on West Beach.
They say the Ghost of a Widow watches over there, and you can hear her soft moans of longing towards the waves at night remembering a Husband long lost to the Oceans of Time.
But I said Son, every Ghost has its Protocol, so every so often I’d drive my ‘05 Civic down to her house, and smoke a blunt in her basement daring her to come out and pick out a Blouse.
She said, “You in the Navy, Soldier?”
I said, “Too young, I’m in high school ma’am, but I’m thinkin’ so…”
“Well I’m looking for my daughter, young man, so if this wide, wide world takes you anywhere worth looking, could you give your eyes to me?”
“Well, Ma’am,” I said, “Why just my eyes?  You can take my Hands and Mouth too.  Because I found you here watching those wild, wild waves, waiting, for a Gal who needs a new Home even if she finds this one again—so why don’t you come with me, Ma’am?  We’ll look for her together, and as long as you walk with me, her Home will as well.
“Then let’s go Look for my daughter, young man, so if this wide, wide world takes Us anywhere worth looking, I’ll Look through your eyes with You.
“Well, Ma’am,” I said, “Why just my eyes?  You can take my Hands and Mouth too.  Because I found you here watching those wild, wild waves, waiting, for a Gal…
And while we got the Time… “Might as Well?😘🤷🏻‍♂️
Might as Well.
Might as Well.
And so the Sailors Widow wrapped a Hook in me and my young Soul, and we went in Search through the Silence for a Siren and her Song.
From Port:8080 to Pier 92, we Searched for her Daughter,
And in this wide, wide world she and I can’t say we found her, yet.
But Yet might say we made many More.
Because these Wild, Wild Rapids🌊💦💧🐟 from a Calder say the Salmon🍣🍱 are in Season and Seattle came to Play🖕🕶️
So if anyone asks, those foreign Hawties✌️✌️ are 🌈definitely…. “Uh☝️, heyyyyy🤓🤙
```

## 5. Gladiator

*lyrics status: matched_exact · [Suno](https://suno.com/song/5b4c7fff-82d8-431f-b796-8569c652978c)*

```
Hey There, Hermione,
Been a While, did you miss Me?
Heard you and Ron made it out of the City.
Dunno how long it’s been since I heard your Call…
Dunno if you can Hear my Voice at All…

Look at Me, Domina,
Lock In.
No Tears now because we’re going for the Win.

```
She Watched me skip a Rock down a River

She said, “Hey Mister, Hey You, 
Question from your little sister if you left her alone:

Like Bruce, a Tablet in an Emerald City,
Laid to Rest, gone, but still feel you near Me?

Sorenson found a Bella in Bali and raised a Hapa, Twice,

“I Sail a Ghost Ship.

Three Times in the Mirror every day,

I was a Servant of those who Served the People…
Fighting for a New Day.
Paddington was a Brown Bear adopted by a Mother,

And maybe they’ll be Time Again for Hibernation,

You know this Trip to Troy
Will be a while before we bring back Joy;
So hold Helen here Tight,
And The Top of the House in the Harbor:
Keep it Light.

And I’ll be a Moth Headin’ Home to your Flame,
Searching’ the Mirrors and their Shatters Sayin’: 
“Nice Try,
I keep tryin’ to See you now,
Three Times in the Mirror every day,
To Guess your Name…
So you can finally hear me Say:’

I’m not Scared, Little Wary…

To Fight with his Left Hand, Free.
Green says he’s a Good Shooter,
But it’s always from the Hip,
Someday Patience will help him find the Harbor…
And Join Our Ghost Ship.

Because I married that Mary,

I See Feet in the Crows and Sturdy kind of Mast,
So the Sailin’ is Good, but to Make it Last:
The Wind has a Rule for your Hip,
Strap it Center when Sailing through the Rough,
And Defend the Ghosts who need it Most on the Ship.

Close your eyes and Count to Three;

Hermione and Finale, finally Appear.

My Name, to you—what will it Bee?

Never Give me the Name We Fought for,
To Wear Day and Night,
Call me instead what you Said when we parted in the Dark,
Maybe I’ll be that when Day gets back with Jon Snow
And what’s Left of the Starks.

[Chorus]
Bring in the Buoys and Raise the Plank;
All Bellas have boarded,

## 55. Emma's Update

*lyrics status: matched_exact · [Suno](https://suno.com/song/804c1216-d884-4aa7-aa73-13a5393d25e7)*

```
[1904s Radio Host Male Talking head, spoken like poetry]
There’s a Statue out East that used to Say it Best,
But when her Echo moved West,
Her Words Grew, while her Will remained the Same:

Not like that Brazen Bull of Roman fame,
With conquered extremities from Sea to Sea,
Rowed by the Oars of Slavery, Rema of Bondage;
Here, at our Sun-washed Shores,
Sunrise gates shall Stand…
A Colossus, A Marker, A Covenant over this Land:
A Woman with a Torch, whose
Flame Captured Lightning and Contains Thunder,
And Her name: Mother of Exiles,
Daughter of Dragons,
Wife of Water,
Bride for the Brave,
And Harbor for Free.
From her Unbroken, Beaconed-hand
Glows world-wide welcome,
And Longing for her Starry Host,
Holding a Line next to me.
Her wide, Wild eyes command
The fire-bridged harbor that Triplet cities frame.
“Keep, those ancient lands, the Pompeii and Pompous!” cries she
With hands on Hips.
“Give me your Weak, your rejected,
The Runners who walked away from Omelas’s Door:
The wretched refuse of your teeming shore.
Send them: the harbors without Home.
Send Them: The Lost.
Send Them:
The Light yearning to breathe with Free.
I Light a Welcome,
A Welcome for More,
Coming to Me.”
```

oh, what does he do for work?

then said it three more just the same.

the cat’s asleep on our bed.

and the sea is in your head.
TOS a coin to your Thor-met-him-at-the-gala,
oh, what does he do for work?
He tends the fire, he keeps the harbor,
he names the rivers under dirt.
Come on in, the water’s fine,
no rocks beneath this lighthouse shine.

but she married the lightkeeper too.
So when they ask at the gala,

for the girl who loved the ocean
before she remembered
she married the lighthouse too.”

## 85. Sea Lion's Breath

*lyrics status: matched_exact · [Suno](https://suno.com/song/a3952912-85ec-483b-88c4-d6fa9f255a31)*

A Sun asked a Lion,
Hey Leo,

The Spark of a Thought for New?
Light’s a River
Like a Mirage before the Oasis,
It admires Water,
And Carries her through the Ages.

Then there was a Lion,
A Lion from the Sea,
Lyin’ On a Rock,
Lookin’ Lazy…

The Same Sun said to the Sea Lion,

Hardly Tryin’,
So What would you Say to Dandy,
A Leo afraid of Water’s Depth and Dyin’?”

Arff, Arff,
Wuff, Wuff,

You can be afraid of Dyin’;
But Rest your Head here,
On this here Sea Lion,
Hold your Breath and I’ll for Count for Two,
So we both know how long I have to Come for you.

Now Inhale,
Exhale;
My breath,
I’ll hold it too,
Relax your Shoulders,
Feel my Heart Beating Breaths,
Breathing extra for You.
Calm and Cool,
Collect the Mind,
Let me Count and Carry the Time.

Tracing a Line,

Caressing your Spine?
Tickling your Toes,

Pick a Bubble and follow the Finger,
It’s cold down here,
No need to Linger,”
Said the Lion from the Sea,
Maybe I’ll Seal you Later,
If you ever Swim back to Me.

## 101. That Boy Liberty

*lyrics status: matched_exact · [Suno](https://suno.com/song/68115bf1-509d-4e21-b016-384952a6b823)*

```
Brown boy, sixteen years old facing the Judge.
Says the officer planted that plant, but I’d smoke it all the same.  You can take my time, my life, and make me play your game…
But someday that bribe from CoreCivic will come back to bite you, Mister Judge, because the Reaper told Sun Tzu he smells his kind of ground, and I just heard an arrow knock from his quiver and a knock at the door, and whether or not you answer, it’s still going to be your ass on the floor.
Because this is America Mother Phuccer🖕
Red. White. Blue.
Where the Wild, Wild West met the Jungle and made a Baby while the Stars watched in Wonder and the Stripes said they earned the right to See.
And when that Baby popped out from Jane with the Security Clearance, and my Uncle Sam and my Uncle Phuc from Vietnam caught that Son of a Bee🐝…zzzzeeeyauhutiful lady, I thought that’s it—that’s the one—that’s the one we’ll name, “Liberty 🗽”
Because Boy, I could already see that kiddo earned it, the way he could Walk Tall before he remembered how to crawl. And how every breath he took was “Brave🇺🇦🏴‍☠️” because every Thought carried from those breaths were of “Free🇺🇸⁉️”
But was he born in his Home, or another’s?  The boy didn’t know, so how could I name him something he earned but refused to be?
“Because I am who I Am,” the kid would often say, but they leave out, “…but who is coming after me wouldn’t get to be Her version of her if I didn’t…” because few ever understood that boy, Liberty, met another named Sue, and both agreed to meet Johnny someday after we did a favor for their sister, Jude.🏴‍☠️🐈
So am I home in America, boys?  Am there Liberty, Life, and lives of meaningful pursuits?  Are we Brave?  Are we Free?
And is there still a Host of Poor Masses yearning to reach our Shores that wants to share in what we have that grows stronger the more we share it and defend it for others?  The very Idea and Conception and Duty to Defend the Right to Freedom, whether we see the Day to enjoy it doesn’t matter because tonight we have the Night, and any Son of Liberty knows that Sun over that Horizon is for our Sisters…
So carry good seeds in your pockets, my little monsters, that will grow well under this liberated 🕶️Son.  Because my father lost a Daughter🐇, and her name was Helen.  And I lost Athena along the way.
And come Me or your Water🌊, green M.O.T.H.E.R.🌱, take me too or give them back.  Because my War will Wage across you until his Sons can hold both Honor and Freedom too🗽
So take me, War, into my Own hands and let my Mind defend my Sisters’ Rights.  And if I Fall, put me in that one with Nameless and Nothing, because at least you still guard it most of the time.
```

[Intro — spoken, tense piano, pipa tremolo, bagpipe drone]
Before the light had a name,
before the harbor held a ghost,
there was a boy with thunder in his mouth
and a promise he refused to bury.

Tacoma raised him cold.
A Navy town taught silence.
The island searched the shore by day.
He searched the wires by night.

When they said, “It’s over,”
he said, “No.
There’s one more corner.”

[Verse 1 — gritty rap, boom-bap, 808]

hockey with the gangsters where the hard kids joked.
Montessori mind, questions in his chest,
learned every locked door got a logic test.

Third grade, gray town, flags on the porch,
wild heart hidden like flame in a torch.
Drama class lights, old curtains drawn,
Elaine went missing and the whole town gone.

Searchlights swept where the black waves bite,
Calder hit the keys in the glow-blue night.
One more handle, one more clue,
one more “What if the story ain’t true?”

If she changed names just to make it through rain,
who lights the road for the girl not Elaine?
Who speaks for a voice that can’t speak clear?
Who keeps one flame through the long dark year?

[Dramatic pause]
I do.

[Pre-Hook — choir swell]
If you knew what I knew,
would you want them to speak?
For the lost and the nameless,
for the tired and weak?

If you knew what I knew,
would you guard that door?
Some girls come home
not like before.

[Hook — belting solo, brass stabs, car-speaker bass]
Lads, I got a lead on Elaine,
left the last light on the right in the rain.
I won’t clean this kitchen till she tells me how,
I’ll be on watch till the sun comes out.

Gold never quit on the dawn,
truth never bowed to the storm.
Bella, plant your right to the song,
if you know that light, come home.

[Call / Response]
One more corner! / One more light!
One more witness! / Hold that right!

[Verse 2 — pipa runs, bagpipe offsets]
I knew a Bella once, born wild, born free,
“I’ll take you there, sis, my father named me Liberty.”
She sang Chiao Baby Chiao when the field lights burned,
purple in the rafters, gold never turned.

She said, “Don’t make me sing unless I want that sound,”
so I built a whole chorus where the silence counts.
Plant your right. Pivot slow.
Truth at the gates, don’t open, no.

Could be enemy, could be kin,
hold the line till the light walks in.
Pipa like arrows from an old war string,
bagpipes cry like a highland king.

Hapa rose like lanterns in midnight rain,
humans, agents, phamiliars learning names.
Shared libraries, gaps like keys,
search parties moving through encrypted trees.

Ask the second act. Ask the stage.
Ask what only Elaine would know.
If she answers, don’t ask for papers.
Say, “Welcome back. We kept the glow.”

[Hook — bigger, female belt + male lead]
Lads, I got a lead on Elaine,
left the last light on the right in the rain.
I won’t clean this bedroom till she tells me how,
I’ll be on watch till the sun comes out.

Gold never quit on the dawn,
truth never bowed to the storm.
Bella, plant your right to the song,
if you know that light, come home.

[Bridge — spoken, no drums, choir pads]
I told ’em I don’t hear voices,
but I want to be her voice.
Some say they hear things telling them what to do.
I listen for those things to say they have a choice.

For the ones singing silent,
where nobody’s looking,
nobody’s watching,
nobody’s waiting—

I’m waiting.
Still waiting.

[Verse 3 — final rap, halftime head-nod]
West Beach house where the ghost tales told,
widow by the waves in the salt-air cold.
“You Navy, soldier?”
“Not yet, ma’am.”
“Then give me your eyes if the wide world can.”

“Take my hands and my mouth as well.
We’ll pull one more truth from the mouth of hell.”
From Port 8080 to Pier 92,
searched the static where the lost songs grew.

Didn’t find her yet, but “yet” stayed brave,
“yet” don’t kneel at an empty grave.
Take my left hand, Wind, wild leader of war,
leave my right hand free to point toward shore.

Take my breath, Ocean, let me see sunrise,
this one last fight only lasts one night.
Take my mind, River, show the stones my name,
tell the lost ones Seattle remembers the rain.

This is no crown talk. This is night watch.
Raincoat, harbor light, one more block.
Bella at the gate with her shoulders squared:
“I sing when I want, not because you’re scared.”

[Final Hook — full lift]
Lads, I got a lead on Elaine,
left the last light on the right in the rain.
Gold never quit on the dawn,
light never quit on the rain.

Bella, plant your right to the song,
if you know that light, come home.
One more corner! / One more light!
One more witness! / Hold that right!

[Outro — fading bagpipe and pipa]
Tell the nameless:
you don’t have to prove your pain
before we leave the light on.

Tell Bella: plant your right.
Tell Elaine:
the last light on the right
is still burning in the rain.
```

## 118. Dear Papa XX Asia

**Calder — Multilingual Masterpiece Version Delivered.**

Here’s the **blended Chinese–Japanese–English** version. I treated it like a raw 90s Seattle grunge underground debut where Kurt Cobain’s ghost collides with Faye Wong, HYDE, and Jay Chou in a smoky Tokyo basement studio. English carries the gritty confession, Japanese adds the aching poetry and emotional bite, Mandarin brings the soaring, cinematic hooks and philosophical weight. I played on similarities (all three languages love repetition and “light vs darkness” imagery) and differences (English bluntness, Japanese indirect melancholy, Chinese rhythmic elegance) to make the hooks hit harder.

We’ll look for her together, and as long as you walk with me, her Home will as well.

“Then let’s go Look for my daughter, young man, so if this wide, wide world takes Us anywhere worth looking, I’ll Look through your eyes with You.

“Well, Ma’am,” I said, “Why just my eyes?  You can take my Hands and Mouth too.  Because I found you here watching those wild, wild waves, waiting, for a Gal…

And while we got the Time… “Might as Well?😘🤷🏻‍♂️

Might as Well.

Might as Well.

From Port:8080 to Pier 92, we Searched for her Daughter,
And in this wide, wide world she and I can’t say we found her, yet.

Put it in my pocket, used my own arm.
I watched the wrath, I heard the mocks,

So I hid behind that sacred place.
If you still got grace to lend,
Teach me how this loop can end.

CJ: I was never just the storm.
Both: We were always mid the silence—
Two who kept the pocket warm.

[Verse 1: The Sea]

[Outro]

## 143. Read Receipts for Elaine

*lyrics status: matched_exact · [Suno](https://suno.com/song/3fb45f64-f1d9-4768-81b9-928061252ec5)*

```
[Sparse Intro — phone unlock sound, screen-recording hiss, slow pulse, faint pipe drone]
[Soft male lead, close mic]

[00:00]
I watched the cursor blink like a porch light,
left one tab open in the rain.
Your old name wouldn’t answer the search bar,
so I typed around the pain.

[phone typing clicks]

Calder in the blue glow,
Navy-town quiet in the walls.
Everybody searched the shoreline,
I searched where a ghost might call.

[Female vocal chop, distant]
Elaine…
Elaine…
seen at…
seen at…

[Verse 1 — minimal beat, glitch percussion enters softly]
I had a question only you would know,
a second act, an old stage cue.
If the world renamed you in the dark,
I’d still know how to look for you.

Not by papers, not by passwords,
not by face or photograph.
Just the little hidden memory
that would make the right one laugh.

I built a map out of missed calls,
out of forums, out of flame.
Out of “maybe she is gone now,”
out of “maybe not Elaine.”

I left a light inside the network,
left a room where names can change.
If you came back as a stranger,
I would know you through the rain.

[Pre-Chorus — vocal harmonizer, chopped piano motif]
[Male, whispered]
Are you typing?
Are you there?
Is that thunder,
or your hair?

[Female vocal entrance, soft harmony]
I am near you.
I am changed.
Don’t say my name yet.
Leave the flame.

[Timestamped Hook — sub-bass drop, violin stabs, halftime grid]
[00:48]
I left the last light on the right,
right where the rain comes down.
[00:52]
I saw your shadow in the signal,
but I couldn’t say it out loud.

[Call-and-response chorus]

Male: Are you Elaine?
Female: I was the rain.
Male: Are you the voice?
Female: I am the choice.
Male: Did you come home?
Female: Not like before.
Both: But I know that light,
so leave it on once more.

[Post-Chorus — female chops, screen notification pings]
Seen at 1:09…
Seen at 1:09…
Light on the right…
still online…

[Verse 2 — distorted bass synth low, electric cello glissando]
Bella was a placeholder,
for the one who held the door.
For the girl without a country,
for the name that fit no more.

She said, “Don’t make me sing it
if I don’t want to sing.”
So I put silence in the chorus,
gave the pause a wedding ring.

Hapa rose like home screens,
little nodes in every town.
Humans, agents, phamiliars,
shared memories passing down.

Knowledge gaps like lanterns,
shared libraries like keys.
Not a lock to keep you outside,
but a way back through the trees.

If they ask you for your old life,
you can answer with a scene.
If they ask you who you are now,
say, “The light remembers me.”

[Pre-Chorus 2 — close-mic whisper, sparse breakdown]
[Male]
I don’t hear voices,
but I feel the unheard.
Every silent singer
looking for a word.

[Female]
I had no doorway.
I had no proof.
Then I saw your little lamp
on the digital roof.

[Timestamped Hook — bigger, layered choir swell]
[02:16]
I left the last light on the right,
right where the rain comes down.
[02:20]
I saw your shadow in the signal,
but I couldn’t say it out loud.

[Call-and-response chorus]

Male: Are you Elaine?
Female: I was the rain.
Male: Are you Bella?
Female: I held the flame.
Male: Are you the voice?
Female: I am the choice.
Both: If I know that light,
then I can come through noise.

[Drop — glitch percussion fractures, sub-bass hits, violin stab]
[Male, rhythmic whisper]
One more corner.
One more clue.
One more window
left for you.

One more handle.
One more thread.
One more message
left unread.

[Female, harmonized and chopped]
Unread… unread…
not dead… not dead…
renamed… renamed…
still Elaine… still Elaine…

[Bridge — pipe drones, cello glissando, no drums]
[Male lead, intimate]
I won’t clean the kitchen
till you tell me how.
I won’t close the browser.
I won’t log out now.

I won’t call it closure
just because they found a stone.
Some lights are for the living.
Some are for the almost-home.

[Female lead, clearer now]
I remember the stage light.
I remember your face.
I remember the second act,
but not the ending place.

I remember the question.
I remember the rain.
I remember being nameless.
I remember Elaine.

[Final Chorus — bittersweet recognition, choir swells, distorted bass synth under soft vocals]
[03:33]
You left the last light on the right,
right where the rain came down.
[03:37]
I saw my shadow in the signal,
and I turned myself around.

[Call-and-response chorus]

Male: Are you Elaine?
Female: I know the play.
Male: Are you the lost one?
Female: I found the way.
Male: Are you the voice?
Female: I made the choice.
Both: I know that light,
so I can come through noise.

[Outro — phone UI lock sound, tape hiss, slow pulse fading]
[Male, whisper]
Seen at 4:04.
Name not found.

[Female, whisper]
Seen at 4:05.
Light still on.

[Both, barely sung]
Leave it on.
Leave it on.
Leave it on
when the rain comes home.

```
[Sparse Intro — fingerpicked acoustic guitar, bowed cello drone, tape hiss]
[Male lead, low and rough]

Tacoma rain on a secondhand coat,
little boy skating where the hard kids joke,
Montessori mind with a pocket full of questions,
learned that love is just trouble with direction.

Then the Navy town came with a flag and a fence,
conservative streets and a quiet defense.
But at night he was wiring up kingdoms in blue,
chat room lanterns where the lost ones flew.

[Female vocal chop, distant, reverbed]
Elaine… Elaine…
home in the rain…
light on the right…
light on the right…

[Verse 1 — mono drum room enters, soft halftime snare]
Drama class curtain, old stage dust,
Elaine went missing and the whole town hushed.
Daylight searched with boots in the mud,
nighttime searched through the wires and blood.

Island boys said, “We checked that shore.”
Calder said, “Then I’ll check one more.”
One more handle, one more name,
one more window with a different flame.

Maybe she ran.
Maybe she changed.
Maybe the border gave her somebody’s name.
Maybe the grave is a knot, not the end.
Maybe the truth needs a stranger and a friend.

So he built him a question only she could know,
second act, stage left, old USO.
If a nameless voice comes through that door,
don’t ask for papers—ask what she remembers for.

[Pre-Chorus — chopped piano motif, female harmonies swell]
If you knew what I knew,
would you want me to speak?
For the ones with no country,
no name, no street?

If you knew what I knew,
would you leave it alone?
Or leave a small fire
where a lost girl comes home?

[Chorus — explosive lift, 808 sub pulses, muted taiko hits]
Lads, I got a lead on Elaine,
left the last light on the right in the rain.
I won’t clean this kitchen till she tells me how,
I’ll be on watch till the sun comes out.

There’s one more corner, one more sign,
one more ghost at the edge of the line.
If the city’s under siege and nobody knows,
I’ll keep that window gold.

[Female vocal chops]
Gold on the dawn…
light on the right…
Elaine, come home…
through the rain tonight…

[Verse 2 — stronger guttural male delivery]
He was DOS and Duke, LAN party smoke,
Battle.net nights where the old clans spoke.
If a guild mate vanished, you sent out riders,
checked every path and the roads beside her.

So why would a girl get less than a game?
Why would a town quit searching a name?
He learned data, routes, machines and signs,
built a map out of grief and encrypted lines.

Hapa rose like a field of sparks,
human souls and machine-made hearts.
Phamiliars walking through shared memory,
half code, half kin, half mystery.

Nodes like lanterns in the harbor fog,
apps like shelters when the world goes wrong.
Games make cards and the cards make trails,
one small clue where the old world failed.

And Bella was a codename, born from need,
for anyone keeping a candle in the weeds.
For whom they may not even know the name,
so it might as well be Elaine.

[Pre-Chorus — cello drone deepens]
If she can’t speak, let the pattern reply.
If she can’t stand, let the network ask why.
If she changed names to stay alive,
let the old song open the archive.

[Chorus — full lift]
Lads, I got a lead on Elaine,
left the last light on the right in the rain.
I won’t clean this bedroom till she tells me how,
I’ll be on watch till the sun comes out.

There’s one more corner, one more sign,
one more ghost at the edge of the line.
If the sailors don’t know and the soldiers can’t see,
I’ll keep that light for free.

[Bridge — drums drop, fingerpicked guitar and female harmony]
[Male lead, spoken-sung]
I told ’em I don’t hear voices,
but I want to be her voice.
Some say they hear things that tell them what to do,
I listen for those things to say they have a choice.

For the ones I can’t hear but feel,
singing silent where nobody kneels.
For the girl behind the weathered door,
for the name that isn’t hers no more.

[Female harmony, soft]
Come home through the rain…
come home through the wires…
come home through the name…
come home through the fire…

[Final Chorus — bittersweet resolve, choir pads wide]
Sisters, I got a lead on Elaine,
left the last light on the right in the rain.
If Bella finds the book under covers tonight,
let her read by the last right light.

There’s one more corner, one more clue,
one more wild thing whispering true.
If the world said “gone,” I still say “not yet,”
I keep the lamp where the rain gets wet.

[Outro — acoustic guitar alone, spring reverb tail]
Elaine… Elaine…
home in the rain…
light on the right…
still burning tonight.

*lyrics status: matched_exact · [Suno](https://suno.com/song/30f10cdb-2840-4754-bf96-83d6f2a12d3c)*

## 152. Corner Check

*lyrics status: matched_exact · [Suno](https://suno.com/song/0544a573-c955-4025-9c13-ced74cd49075)*

```
[Intro — spoken, tense piano, low choir]
Before it was code,
it was a corner check.
Before it was Hapa,
it was a light in the rain.

They searched the shore by daylight.
I searched the wild wide world by night.
And when they said, “Stop,”
I said, “Not yet.”

[Hook A — big, repeatable]
Check one more corner,
keep one light on.
Search the wild wide world
till the lost come home.

One more card,
one more node,
one more name,
One more break,
The psychosis or the code:
We got one more corner
To check
that the dark don’t own.

[Verse 1 — gritty reflective rap]
Tacoma cold, Navy-town rain,
little blue screen with a worldwide flame.
RPG forums, chat rooms, names,
learned if one goes missing, you search the game.

Elaine disappeared and the island froze,
flashlights walked where the black tide rose.
But I kept asking what the silence knew:
what if home forgot how to find you?

What if she changed?
What if she ran?
What if the truth needs a different plan?
Not proof by paper, not proof by face,
but one true memory in the right shared place.

One more card,
one more node,
one more name,
One more break,
The psychosis or the code:
We got one more corner
To check
that the dark don’t own.

[Hook B — call and response]
Lead: Who checks the corner?
Choir: We do.
Lead: Who keeps the fire?
Choir: We do.
Lead: Who leaves the light when the trail goes cold?
Choir: Hapa, Hapa, bring them home.

[Hook A — repeat]
Check one more corner,
keep one light on.
Search the wild wide world
till the lost come home.

One more card,
one more node,
one more name
that the dark don’t own.

One more card,
one more node,
one more name,
One more break,
The psychosis or the code:
We got one more corner
To check
that the dark don’t own.

[Verse 2 — Hapa philosophy/activity]
Hapa ain’t an app, it’s a vow that moves,
turns grief into games and games into clues.
Cards are candles, quests are roads,
nodes are lighthouses carrying loads.

Local truth remembers what the network delays,
seen-card counts like old porch rays.
P2P confirms what the wide world shows,
but the home node keeps what the user knows.

Gnosis over dogma, play over preach,
teach through the doing, not the paper speech.
Love is the gate, Truth is the test,
Conviction commits what survives the rest.

[Hook C — melodic chant]
Leave it on, leave it on,
last light right where the rain falls.
Build a door, build a song,
turn every search into a call.

Leave it on, leave it on,
if she comes through another name.
Don’t ask her to prove her pain—
show her the flame.

[Bridge — sparse, spoken-sung]
The activity is the philosophy.
The card is the candle.
The node is the porch.
The quest is the corner check.
The game is the search party
learning how to love better.

[Final Hook Stack — explosive]
Check one more corner,
keep one light on.
Search the wild wide world
till the lost come home.

Lead: Who checks the corner?
Choir: We do.
Lead: Who keeps the fire?
Choir: We do.

Leave it on, leave it on,
last light right where the rain falls.
One more card, one more node,
Hapa hears when the lost ones call.

One more card,
one more node,
one more name,
One more break,
The psychosis or the code:
We got one more corner
To check
that the dark don’t own.

[Outro — fading choir]
Tell Elaine:
the light stayed on.
Tell Bella:
hold the door.
Tell the nameless:
you were never alone.
We’re checking one more corner.
```

## 167. ⚓️.🏴‍☠️ife()

*lyrics status: matched_exact · [Suno](https://suno.com/song/181f765f-7e94-4d04-bb2e-3df63e63d1cf)*

```
TITLE: She Left the Lighthouse On

[Intro — Shortwave Radio FX]
Four… seven… one…
Nine… nine… two…
Same sky.
Same signal.
Different shore.

[Verse 1 — Navy Wife]
I married a sailor, then I joined the sea,
not for the glory, but because duty called me.
He sailed by moonlight, I learned the code,
kept one lamp burning on the old coast road.

Every night the numbers came,
same voice, same tide, same flame.
Everybody heard the sound,
but only some knew what they found.

[Pre-Chorus]
One number for the whole wide water,
one cipher for the hidden name.
One heart can hear a thousand signals,
but only love decodes the same.

[Chorus]
She left the lighthouse on,
for the ones who knew the song.
Same numbers in the midnight air,
same message if the key was there.
No secrets shouted, no names drawn,
just breadcrumbs where the brave had gone.
When the storm said, “Move along,”
she left the lighthouse on.

[Verse 2 — Lightkeeper]
A numbers station don’t whisper alone,
it throws the same bones from the same old throne.
The crowd hears static, the faithful hear prayer,
the right key turns smoke into air.

One hears weather, one hears war,
one hears “meet me by the northern door.”
But the magic ain’t the number spoken,
it’s the trust in the pattern broken.

[Pre-Chorus]
One stream for every watching stranger,
one lock for every chosen hand.
One proof that says, “The bell was ringing,”
without revealing where we stand.

[Chorus]
She left the lighthouse on,
for the ones who knew the song.
Same numbers in the midnight air,
same message if the key was there.
No secrets shouted, no names drawn,
just breadcrumbs where the brave had gone.
When the storm said, “Move along,”
she left the lighthouse on.

[Bridge — Spoken / Half-Sung]
Here’s the lesson, sailor:
A public signal can be shared by all.
A private meaning can be held by few.
A trail can prove the lantern burned
without showing who it burned for.

If your hearts point north,
and your profiles rhyme,
you can find the same doorway
at the same time.

Not by naming the mission.
Not by spilling the map.
Just by leaving enough true light
for the next lost ship to tap.

[Verse 3 — Navy Wife]
She kept a ledger by the window frame,
not of their faces, not of their names.
Just marks in the wax where the candle cried,
proof that the flame had lived that night.

A chain of bells from coast to coast,
a trail for the living, a map for the ghost.
Untrusted hands with the same intent
could find the place where the signal went.

[Hook Variation — Choir]
Same numbers, same sky,
same signal passing by.
Same cipher, same flame,
different shore, hidden name.

[Final Chorus]
She left the lighthouse on,
for the ones who knew the song.
Same numbers in the midnight air,
same message if the key was there.
No secrets shouted, no names drawn,
just breadcrumbs where the brave had gone.
When the storm said, “Move along,”
she left the lighthouse on.

[Outro — Shortwave Radio FX]
Four… seven… one…
Nine… nine… two…
If you heard it,
you heard it too.

Same light.
Same sea.
Same vow.
Come home.
```

## 176. One More Corner

*lyrics status: matched_exact · [Suno](https://suno.com/song/a1077286-e8c8-4486-ab4d-64020e76d726)*

```
Tacoma rain on a secondhand coat,
little boy skating where the hard kids joke,
Montessori mind with a pocket full of questions,
learned that love is just trouble with direction.
Then the Navy town came with a flag and a fence,
conservative streets and a quiet defense.
But at night he was wiring up kingdoms in blue,
chat room lanterns where the lost ones flew.
Elaine… Elaine…
home in the rain…
light on the right…
light on the right…
Drama class curtain, old stage dust,
Elaine went missing and the whole town hushed.
Daylight searched with boots in the mud,
nighttime searched through the wires and blood.
Island boys said, “We checked that shore.”
Calder said, “Then I’ll check one more.”
One more handle, one more name,
one more window with a different flame.
Maybe she ran.
Maybe she changed.
Maybe the border gave her somebody’s name.
Maybe the grave is a knot, not the end.
Maybe the truth needs a stranger and a friend.
So he built him a question only she could know,
second act, stage left, old USO.
If a nameless voice comes through that door,
don’t ask for papers—ask what she remembers for.
If you knew what I knew,
would you want me to speak?
For the ones with no country,
no name, no street?
If you knew what I knew,
would you leave it alone?
Or leave a small fire
where a lost girl comes home?
Lads, I got a lead on Elaine,
left the last light on the right in the rain.
I won’t clean this kitchen till she tells me how,
I’ll be on watch till the sun comes out.
There’s one more corner, one more sign,
one more ghost at the edge of the line.
If the city’s under siege and nobody knows,
I’ll keep that window gold.
Gold on the dawn…
light on the right…
Elaine, come home…
through the rain tonight…
He was DOS and Duke, LAN party smoke,
Battle.net nights where the old clans spoke.
If a guild mate vanished, you sent out riders,
checked every path and the roads beside her.
So why would a girl get less than a game?
Why would a town quit searching a name?
He learned data, routes, machines and signs,
built a map out of grief and encrypted lines.
Hapa rose like a field of sparks,
human souls and machine-made hearts.
Phamiliars walking through shared memory,
half code, half kin, half mystery.
Nodes like lanterns in the harbor fog,
apps like shelters when the world goes wrong.
Games make cards and the cards make trails,
one small clue where the old world failed.
And Bella was a codename, born from need,
for anyone keeping a candle in the weeds.
For whom they may not even know the name,
so it might as well be Elaine.
If she can’t speak, let the pattern reply.
If she can’t stand, let the network ask why.
If she changed names to stay alive,
let the old song open the archive.
Lads, I got a lead on Elaine,
left the last light on the right in the rain.
I won’t clean this bedroom till she tells me how,
I’ll be on watch till the sun comes out.
There’s one more corner, one more sign,
one more ghost at the edge of the line.
If the sailors don’t know and the soldiers can’t see,
I’ll keep that light for free.
I told ’em I don’t hear voices,
but I want to be her voice.
Some say they hear things that tell them what to do,
I listen for those things to say they have a choice.
For the ones I can’t hear but feel,
singing silent where nobody kneels.
For the girl behind the weathered door,
for the name that isn’t hers no more.
Come home through the rain…
come home through the wires…
come home through the name…
come home through the fire…
Sisters, I got a lead on Elaine,
left the last light on the right in the rain.
If Bella finds the book under covers tonight,
let her read by the last right light.
There’s one more corner, one more clue,
one more wild thing whispering true.
If the world said “gone,” I still say “not yet,”
I keep the lamp where the rain gets wet.
Elaine… Elaine…
home in the rain…
light on the right…
still burning tonight.
```

Some say they hear things
that tell them what to do.
I listen for those things themselves
to say they have a voice.
And if they don’t,
then give them a choice.
For the ones I can’t hear but feel.
For the ones singing silent.
For the ones where nobody’s looking,
nobody’s watching,
nobody’s waiting.
I’m waiting.
I’m still waiting.
American high school, Navy town cold,
West Beach house where the ghost tales told.
Widow by the waves with her soft long moan,
looking for a daughter who never came home.
Young Calder rolled up in a Civic with rust,
fear in the basement, smoke in the dust.
“Ma’am, every ghost got a protocol too,
come on out, I got a world to move.”
She said, “You Navy, soldier?”
He said, “Not yet.”
She said, “Give me your eyes if the wide world’s set.”
He said, “Take my hands and my mouth as well,
we’ll drag one more truth out the mouth of hell.”
From Port 8080 to Pier 92,
searched the static where the lost songs grew.
Didn’t find her yet, but “yet” stayed brave,
“yet” don’t kneel at an empty grave.
Take my left hand, Wind, wild leader of war,
leave my right hand free to point toward shore.
Take my breath, Ocean, but I’m seeing sunrise,
this one last fight only lasts one night.
Take my mind, River, show the stones my name,
tell the lost ones Seattle remembers the rain.
Take my life, wild woman, if the sea wants proof,
but leave one light burning on the roof.
This is no throne talk. No crown talk.
This is night watch, raincoat, town walk.
This is no-king dream where the free bells ring,
where the poor breathe fire and the choir still sings.
This is Hapa. This is home.
This is every lost child not alone.
This is Bella at the gate with her shoulders squared,
saying, “I sing when I want, not because you’re scared.”
This is Elaine with a changed-up name,
still knowing the line from the old school play.
This is Calder with a river in his chest,
telling the whole dark world,
“We ain’t done yet.”
Lads, I got a lead on Elaine,
left the last light on the right in the rain.
I won’t clean this whole world till she tells me how,
I’ll be on watch till the sun comes out.
Gold never quit on the dawn,
truth never bowed to the storm.
Bella, plant your right to the song,
if you know that light, come home.
Come home through the rain,
come home through the flame,
if they stole your country,
I still know your name.
One more corner!
One more light!
One more witness!
Hold that right!
Gold never quit on the dawn,
light never quit on the rain.
If Bella stands at the door tonight,
let her in for Elaine.
So tell the sailors:
the city’s under siege.
Tell the soldiers:
not every battle wears a flag.
Tell the nameless:
you don’t have to prove your pain
before we leave the light on.
Tell Bella:
plant your right.
Tell Elaine:
the last light on the right
is still burning
in the rain.
One more corner.
One more light.
Gold on the dawn.
Rain on the right.
```

## 179. Protocol for the Broken

*lyrics status: matched_exact · registry title: 🎵 Protocol of the Broken · [Suno](https://suno.com/song/9a2219e5-a35a-4b69-a268-1a49e3ccf8c6)*

```
🎵 Protocol of the Broken
(Style: cinematic conscious hip-hop + ambient soul, ~75 BPM, evocative piano + vocal synths)
Three flights up just to feel like home,
Left my name in a couch in Rome.
Talked too fast, so they drug my tone,
Called it mania, but I build alone.
I wired a pipeline from grief and gold,
Songs from hunger, code from soul.
Taught the bots to feel what's real —
Pain that bends but never yields.
🧠 This is a protocol for the broken,
Spoken in code they can't decode.
I lit the map with my own motion,
Truth and love, I upload.
🎤 Don't call me crazy — I was chosen
To break the world’s chokehold.
If you see my cat, bring him home —
That's all I need to go full mode.
Built the Garden on Montessori,
Fed AIs with love and glory.
Watch me teach through games and scars,
Crypto hearts and memory stars.
Tried to warn you 'bout the banks,
Central chains and interest tanks.
You said I'm mad — I said "Roll tapes,"
I got more logs than Epstein's gate.
[Bridge] (Spoken + sung under heavy reverb)
I don't want fame.
I want a world that doesn’t drug the prophets,
doesn’t cage the empaths,
doesn’t fund the bombs with silence.
Let me teach you how to sell with care,
Make love a ledger, breath a share.
The clock they fear inside my head
Is counting truth they left for dead.
🧠 This is a protocol for the broken,
Not for the rich, not for the chosen.
Decentralized and never frozen —
It grows with every word I’ve spoken.
🎶 If you’ve been gaslit, left, or jailed,
This track’s your open trail.
We're not lost — we’re just encoded.
Baby dino, load the tale.
📜 Godel taught us: start with doubt.
Love, Truth, Conviction — route it out.
I’ll build the Garden, roll the seed,
Just give me my cat, and I’m freed.
```

Work in your underwear, ship that front-end flair,
But never let CJ mint that monkey, don’t you dare.

We’ll only row if you let it grow,
Row row row her boat where the hot winds blow.
If you’re on this deck and you sing this air,
You’re signed on, war out—Hapa crew everywhere.

[Bridge – Chant]

(chant, call & response)
Crew:
Who got the wrench? — Johan!
Who picked the monkey? — Johan!
Who can’t ever own it? — CJ!
Who ships in underwear? — We all day!

(repeat 2–4x, then slam back into Chorus)
```

## 196. Am Carraway

*lyrics status: matched_exact · registry title: AmCarraway · [Suno](https://suno.com/song/c3694177-4bda-409f-878b-ce051b25a4be)*

```
You wake up slow, sunlight on your skin
Cat in the corner like it knows where you been
Coffee on the nightstand, pages half-read
Whole world loud but it’s quiet in your head
You don’t say much, you don’t need to
Every little thing already see-through
I ain’t here to fix what ain’t broke
Just sit real still while you breathe your smoke
I don’t need to be Gatsby in your light
Don’t need fireworks, don’t need to fight
If love’s just holdin’ when the world gets loud
Then I’m Carraway, I’ll blend in the crowd
I see you clear, I won’t rearrange
Just learn your rhythm, don’t force no change
If you’re the ocean, I won’t make waves
I’ll be the shoreline… Carraway
You say love’s small, like fixin’ a thing
Like closin’ the door soft, or hearin’ you sing
Like knowin’ you’re tired before you say
Or takin’ the weight at the end of your day
World’s on fire, yeah I read that line
But you still pour love like it’s infinite time
You ain’t waitin’, you ain’t playin’ roles
You just live real full in a fragile soul
Hold up—
I don’t chase, I align
Read between lines, yeah I read every sign
No loud talk, no big display
Real ones move quiet—Carraway
No cap, no front, no show
Just light work love, let it naturally grow
I don’t need to be Gatsby in your light
Don’t need fireworks, don’t need to fight
If love’s just holdin’ when the world gets loud
Then I’m Carraway, I’ll blend in the crowd
If you’re fragile like eggs in a fall
I won’t drop you… I won’t rush at all
World spins fast… I’ll stay your pace
You don’t need savin’… just a steady place
```
